# MS.990 Parallel Edition: Canvases 185–214

## Page 214 — IIIF Canvas #185

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0185.JP2` (5890 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0185.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0185.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,850 / 2,350

Page 214 concludes the section on excessive uterine bleeding during fever, recommending ligatures on the fingers, cupping glasses on the spleen and liver, and an electuary of rose sugar, blackthorn root, terra sigillata, and red rose juice. A major red rubric then introduces 'Spots, Flea-like Pustules, Petechiae, and Eruptions in this Fever,' accompanied by a colored inset illustration of a seated nude youth covered in red spots. The text distinguishes contagious fever spots caused by poisoned air from children's smallpox/measles ('Durchschlächten'), detailing their colors and prognostic significance.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Zu der 11 sagen** (`#xywh=350,500,2295,1800`): Colored ink and watercolor illustration on the left side of the page showing a nude youth wearing a red cap, seated on a rectangular block in an outdoor landscape, with red spots and pustules (petechiae/pox) distributed across his chest, arms, legs, and face.

## Page 215 — IIIF Canvas #186

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0186.JP2` (5726 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0186.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0186.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,850 / 2,400

Page 215, laid out in two columns, presents the treatment for fever pustules, petechiae, and smallpox-like eruptions. The left column warns against chilling the patient or overheating them with excessive bedclothes, and gives two detailed Latin apothecary recipes: a precious-stone cordial powder (bezoardic/cordial powder with pearls, corals, bole, ivory, dittany, camphor, zedoary, saffron, and behen) and a gilded electuary (conserve of roses, borage, bugloss, theriac, mithridate, gold leaf, and citron syrup). The right column offers topical and supportive remedies: lentil and fig waters with vinegar, white rose oil and Solomon's seal for facial skin, juniper-quince salves for ulcerating pustules, and woman's milk, rose honey, or pomegranate gargles for pustules in the mouth, eyes, nose, and throat.

## Page 216 — IIIF Canvas #187

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0187.JP2` (5902 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0187.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0187.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 2,500 / 3,100

Treatise on common hot and malignant fevers ('Von den Gemeinen Hizigen fieber'), describing their humoral causes (yellow bile, poisonous humors entering the blood), pulse characteristics (using Latin sphygmological terms such as intercidentes, vermiculantes, caprizantes, and formicantes), diagnostic signs, and therapeutic goals centered on early emetics, clysters, and diaphoretic (sweat-inducing) prescriptions using hartshorn, diaphoretic antimony, cinnabar of antimony, theriac, and herbal waters. No illustrations.

### Left Column

| Line | German Diplomatic (Original) | English Translation & Editorial Notes |
| :---: | :--- | :--- |

| 2 | fieber etc. | Fevers, etc. |
| 3 | Hie schreib ich von dem gemeinen hizigen fieber welcheß | Here I write of the common hot fever, which |
| 4 | von dem über fluß der gelben gall herkhumbt. daß gabs | comes from the overflow of the yellow bile. That gives |

| 9 | der in das geblüet gieht. derselbige verderbt | which goes into the blood. The same corrupts |

## Page 217 — IIIF Canvas #188

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0188.JP2` (5722 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0188.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0188.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 2,500 / 3,300

Continuation of fever therapeutics: diaphoretic and cordial prescriptions (including volatile salt of hartshorn, occidental and oriental bezoar, pearls, corals, and juleps), cautions against late bloodletting and purging, rules for maintaining gentle warmth and sweat, and management of critical signs (buboes, spots, nosebleeds, diarrhea, and relapse prevention). Begins a new major section 'Von Underschiedlichen Zuefälen So sich bey den hizigen fieber begeben' ('Of Various Symptoms Occurring in Hot Fevers'), starting with severe headache remedies. No illustrations.

## Page 218 — IIIF Canvas #189

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0189.JP2` (5774 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0189.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0189.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 2,500 / 3,400

Continuation of symptom-specific remedies for hot fevers in a full-page layout: treatments for lethargy/sleeping sickness ('Schlaffsucht'), constant wakefulness/insomnia ('Stetes Wachen', warning against internal opium), poultices for the heart, pulse, and soles of the feet, delirium, the patient's ordinary hartshorn drink, pleurisy/lung inflammation with a pectoral decoction, hiccups ('Schlugutzen') and nausea, and excessive/painful urination. No illustrations.

## Page 221 — IIIF Canvas #192

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0192.JP2` (5767 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0192.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0192.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,250 / 2,200

Single-column medical manuscript page (p. 221, verso) containing six therapeutic recipes for consumptive patients ('die dörrsichtigen'), including four medicinal baths (using hazelnut ash lye, goat's milk, sheep's and calves' feet, mallows, and herbs) and two nourishing, healing distilled waters and jellies.

## Page 223 — IIIF Canvas #193

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0193.JP2` (5972 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0193.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0193.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,850 / 2,400

Remedies for hectic fever (hectica) and wasting/consumption (abnemen), followed by a colored anatomical illustration of a man pulling open his chest to reveal the heart and lungs, and an introductory treatise on pulmonary phthisis (Lungelsucht / Tisisie), its contagion, dietary regimen, and initial honey-based electuary.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Illustration 1 (Canvas 193)** (`#xywh=350,500,2336,1800`): Colored ink and wash anatomical illustration in the lower left column depicting a bare-chested man wearing a yellow-ochre cap with brown trim, using both hands to pull open a diamond-shaped incision in his chest, exposing the red heart, lungs, and ribcage.

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0194.JP2` (5753 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0194.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0194.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,850 / 2,350

Continuation of recipes for lung-sickness and consumption (Lungelsucht / Dörrsucht), including electuaries with marshmallow and elecampane, a secretum of ground ivy juice and flowers of tin/sulphur, pectoral powders, snail broth, a cooling syrup for hemoptysis, cynoglossa pills, a goat's milk gruel, and a chest and lung powder.

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0195.JP2` (5962 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0195.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0195.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,850 / 2,450

A pectoral decoction (Decoctum Pectoralis), powders, turpentine wash, and electuaries for lung-sickness and hemoptysis, a spice powder (Triet) for consumptives, followed by a numbered list of five further causes of Lungelsucht and the beginning of diagnostic signs (Zaichen ob ainer ain lungelsucht hat).

## Page 226 — IIIF Canvas #196

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0196.JP2` (5715 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0196.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0196.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,800 / 2,400

Folio 226v continues a treatise on pulmonary consumption (Lungensucht / phthisis), detailing prognostic signs (purulent sputum, night sweats, urine sediment, hair loss, diarrhea, and angina) and presenting several compound remedies including Balsam of Sulfur, Tincture of Tin/Jupiter, Saffron Tincture, Venetian turpentine pills, lozenges to correct acrimony, and a decoction/water for consumptive patients.

### Full Page

| :---: | :--- | :--- |

## Page 227 — IIIF Canvas #197

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0197.JP2` (5892 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0197.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0197.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,800 / 2,600

Folio 227r is laid out in two columns. The left column continues the distilled pulmonary water recipe from f. 226v, adds a marshmallow-iris julep, warns against strong purgatives for consumptives (offering a mild laxative drink instead), and gives a laudanum/cynoglossum pill remedy for children's whooping cough. The right column concludes the pill recipe and presents a 'wonderful secretum' (aloes compress on the stomach), a 'precious lung drink' of herbs and raisins, 'Another' calf's-lung distillation, and a gold-leaf secretum for consumption.

## Page 228 — IIIF Canvas #198

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0198.JP2` (5746 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0198.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0198.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,800 / 2,700

Folio 228v opens the chapter 'Von Bluet speiben' (On Spitting of Blood / Hemoptysis) in two columns. The upper-left features a colored watercolor illustration of a woman vomiting/spitting blood into a bowl. The left column explains the causes, diagnostic distinctions (lung vs. chest vs. stomach vs. head bleeding), and prognosis of hemoptysis. The right column discusses fever-related risks, bloodletting (arm, hand, foot, and leeches to the hemorrhoidal vein), dietary regimen, and three remedies: turpentine/Indian balsam pills with China/sarsaparilla decoction, a 'wonderful electuary' of henbane, poppy, terra sigillata, and red coral, and a 'special remedy' of crab ashes dissolved in vinegar.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Illustration above 'Von Bluet speiben' (f. 228v, upper left)** (`#xywh=850,500,2223,1800`): Colored ink and watercolor illustration depicting a woman in 17th-century attire (headscarf/cap with red ribbons, yellow bodice over a white chemise, red skirt) leaning forward and holding a dark ceramic bowl into which she is spitting/vomiting a stream of bright red blood.

## Page 229 — IIIF Canvas #199

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0199.JP2` (5903 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0199.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0199.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,350 / 2,650

A 17th-century German and Latin medical manuscript page (folio 229r) containing remedies for haemorrhage and haemoptysis (spitting of blood). The left column presents several astringent powders, a secret remedy using eggshell membrane and plantain water, boluses, and a 'wonderful effect' recipe using mouse or rat dung. The right column continues with purgative/astringent instructions using rhubarb, oxymel, and theriac, followed by a decoction for spitting of blood, a chemical preparation of 'Balsam ♃' (balsam of sulphur/tin with turpentine), and a final section on treating haemoptysis with rhubarb, linden charcoal, and blessed thistle water.

## Page 230 — IIIF Canvas #200

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0200.JP2` (5730 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0200.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0200.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,350 / 2,450

Folio 230 verso from Wellcome Collection MS.990 (c. 1675), containing medical notes and recipes in Early New High German with Latin pharmaceutical terminology. The left column concludes a remedy for disordered blood (recommending rhubarb to draw water from the blood), followed by an ink-and-watercolor illustration of a patient inhaling medicinal vapors beneath a linen tent over a brazier. Below the illustration, a section titled 'Fluß Chatar' defines catarrh as phlegm and moisture rising from the stomach, spleen, liver, or womb into the head like vapors in an alembic, listing resulting ailments (apoplexy, abscesses, ear/eye/tooth pain, angina, suffocative catarrh, gout, sciatica). The right column continues the typology of cold and hot catarrhs, prescribes universal purges (emetics, Hauptpillen, syrups of betony, stoechas, calendula, sage, rosemary, violets, chicory), and provides two recipes in red-headed sections: a fumigation ('Volgenter Rauch Ist Köstlich') with white amber, mastic, cloves, and masterwort, and a medicated head fomentation/cap ('Bäthe auf das haubt für Catar... f. Scuffia') with hartshorn, barley, bran, marjoram, betony, red roses, Arabian stoechas, camel's hay, fennel, sweet flag, and mastic.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Fluß Chatar** (`#xywh=850,500,2215,1800`): Watercolor and ink illustration of a figure wearing a white linen cloth draped like a tent over the head and shoulders, wearing a red bodice and white sleeves, leaning over an ochre table to inhale vapors rising from a dark footed brazier or chafing dish filled with glowing coals and medicinal substances.

### Left Column

| Line | German Diplomatic (Original) | English Translation & Editorial Notes |
| :---: | :--- | :--- |

## Page 231 — IIIF Canvas #201

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0201.JP2` (5971 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0201.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0201.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,850 / 2,650

Folio 231 recto from Wellcome MS.990 (c. 1675) presents German and Latin medical recipes and clinical commentary in two columns. The left column contains four fumigation (Rauch) recipes for hot and cold catarrhs and fluxes of the head, followed by an electuary and mutton-broth remedy for prolonged coughing and wheezing, and begins a section on hot catarrh (Von den Hitzigen Catarrn). The right column continues the treatment of hot catarrh—discussing bloodletting, a rhubarb-tamarind purgative, laudanum opiatum, and chest syrups (including coltsfoot), as well as hoarseness—before introducing a new major section on coughing (Von dem Huesten), detailing its physiology, causes (phlegm from the head, vapors from liver, stomach, spleen, or womb, and lung ulcers), and therapeutic cautions.

## Page 232 — IIIF Canvas #202

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0202.JP2` (5743 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0202.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0202.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 3,500 / 2,800

Continuation of cough remedies including elecampane preparations, hot-cough electuaries, children's cough remedies, and external chest salves, followed by a section on great thirst ('Von Großen Durst') accompanied by a watercolor illustration of a woman drinking from a pitcher, and cooling waters/juleps for thirst.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Von Großen Durst.** (`#xywh=850,500,2221,1800`): Ink and watercolor illustration of a woman in profile facing left, wearing a red bodice with white sleeves and a yellow-trimmed headdress, holding a large grey pewter or silver pitcher to her mouth with both hands to drink, standing on a green ground.

## Page 233 — IIIF Canvas #203

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0203.JP2` (5975 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0203.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0203.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 3,500 / 3,200

Treatise on diseases of the heart ('Von kranckheit des Herzens') with an anatomical heart illustration, detailing the causes and cure of cardiac weakness (including a recipe for distilled bull's-heart broth), followed by numerous recipes for heart-strengthening drinks, boluses, cordial pills, heart compresses/poultices, and heart-strengthening salves/sachets.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Illustration 1 (Canvas 203)** (`#xywh=350,500,2337,1800`): Watercolor illustration of a red anatomical heart positioned above a rectangular ink-drawn frame at the top of the left column.

Continuation of heart-strengthening remedies, including compresses, electuaries, heart salves, and cordials with precious stones and magisteries. The lower half introduces a section on heart palpitation and trembling ('Von dem Herz klopffen vnd Zitern') beside a large watercolor illustration of a horizontal anatomical heart, followed by remedies against palpitations.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Illustration 1 (Canvas 204)** (`#xywh=850,500,2216,1800`): Large watercolor illustration of an anatomical heart oriented horizontally with its apex pointing left, located in the lower-left quadrant beside the treatise on heart palpitation.

## Page 235 — IIIF Canvas #205

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0205.JP2` (5964 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0205.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0205.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 3,500 / 2,200

Folio 235r (two columns) continues a section on syncope/fainting and cardiac weakness in the left column, prescribing external applications, bloodletting, internal cordials, two heart ointments ('Xolgendes Sälbel'), and cordial emulsions ('Emulsian Cordial'). The right column opens with a recipe for precious heart lozenges ('Andere Khöstliche Hertz Zeltlen') followed by a clinical treatise on 'Prust Apostem' (thoracic aposteme/empyema), detailing its symptoms, differential diagnosis, causes, and prognosis.

## Page 236 — IIIF Canvas #206

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0206.JP2` (5791 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0206.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0206.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 3,500 / 2,100

Folio 236v (two columns) continues the prognosis and treatment of breast aposteme/empyema. The left column provides emollient poultices and plasters, a pectoral decoction ('Prust Tranckh'), and a linctus ('Zum Leckhen Dis Xolgendes'). The right column describes surgical thoracentesis (incising between the third and fourth ribs to drain pus gradually), a supplementary linseed/sugar remedy, and concludes with a section on 'Prust Wassersucht' (hydrothorax / dropsy of the breast).

## Page 237 — IIIF Canvas #207

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0207.JP2` (5991 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0207.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0207.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 3,500 / 2,100

Folio 237r (two columns) features a large hand-colored sickbed illustration at the top of the left column, followed by a treatise 'Von den Schmerzlichen Seiden Stechen' (Of the Painful Side-Stitches / Pleurisy). The text defines true pleurisy (pleuritis exquisita / legitima) as an inflammation of the pleura and lists its five cardinal symptoms. The right column continues the symptoms, prognostic signs (including conversion to empyema after 14 days and the critical 7th day), and introduces false pleurisy ('Das falsche Seiden stich Spuria oder Bastärdt').

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Illustration 1 (Canvas 207)** (`#xywh=350,500,2345,1800`): Hand-colored gouache/watercolor sickbed scene in the upper left column: a pale, bearded patient lies propped up in a wooden bed under white-and-blue-shaded sheets. A physician or attendant in a green coat stands beside the bed offering a red chalice/cup of medicine, while a woman in a red bodice and dark skirt looks in through an open doorway on the right. Below the scene is a blank rectangular cartouche framed in gold.

## Page 238 — IIIF Canvas #208

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0208.JP2` (5720 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0208.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0208.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 2,100 / 2,650

Medical treatise on pleurisy (Seitenstechen), discussing humoral causes (wind, bile, phlegm, melancholy), diagnostic signs from expectoration and pulse/fever, critical days (4th, 7th, and 9th), and therapeutic regimens including clysters, bloodletting on the affected side, cupping, gentle laxatives (cassia, manna), internal warming oils, and external poultices and fomentations.

## Page 235 — IIIF Canvas #210

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0210.JP2` (5772 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0210.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0210.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 2,100 / 2,700

Further recipes for pleurisy and apostemes, organized by time and day of illness: bedtime juices (licorice, maidenhair, violet julep, red poppy), oriental bezoar, fomentations and pectoral ointments for days 3–4 to mature and break the aposteme, safe chamomile-and-sugar-candy draughts for day 4, sweet almond oil remedies, and soothing plasters/cataplasms with marshmallow, cabbage leaves, and fenugreek.

## Page 236 — IIIF Canvas #211

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0211.JP2` (5954 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0211.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0211.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,850 / 2,150

Page 236 (recto) begins with a powder recipe for pleurisy (Seiden Stöchen), followed by a painted profile portrait of a bearded man above a blank cartouche. Below the illustration, a new section on asthma (Asthma, oder Engbrüstigkeit) discusses its humoral causes, symptoms, diagnostic distinction between cold and heat, and remedies including vesicatories, cupping, fox-lung powder, Elixir proprietatis, a conserve, and a tincture.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Blank cartouche beneath profile portrait of a man** (`#xywh=350,500,2327,1800`): Watercolour and ink profile portrait facing left of a man with curly brown hair, a dark upturned moustache, and a small chin beard, set against a pale blue wash above an empty rectangular cartouche.

## Page 237 — IIIF Canvas #212

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0212.JP2` (5758 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0212.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0212.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,850 / 2,250

Page 237 (verso) continues the treatise on asthma with recipes for currant-and-ginger decoction, lozenges (troches), conserves, pills of woodlice and benzoin, balsam of benzoin, laudanum opiatum, and antimonial balsam. It also includes clinical notes on whether vomiting and sweating are advisable in asthma, concluding with a recipe for a sacratum.

Page 238 (recto) concludes the sacratum recipe and adds pills and a mead decoction for clearing phlegm, followed by a short treatise on dropsy of the chest (hydrothorax / brust Waßer sucht). The right column features a watercolour illustration of a man lying supine in a landscape, followed by emergency resuscitation instructions for suffocation from smoke, drowning, or hanging.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Man lying prostrate in a landscape** (`#xywh=350,500,2324,1800`): Watercolour and ink illustration showing a bearded man in a red coat/tunic, dark breeches, and black shoes lying supine on grassy green ground with a winding path and dark green trees/bushes in the background.

## Page 239 — IIIF Canvas #214

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0214.JP2` (5768 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0214.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0214.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 2,800 / 2,200

Page 239 (verso) opens the section on diseases of the stomach ('Von des Magen Kranckheiten'). At the top of the left column is a red-wash anatomical illustration of the stomach above an empty cartouche. The text discusses the humoral causes of stomach ailments (viscous phlegm, bile, melancholy, heat, and cold), the physiological role of the pylorus and stomach ferment in digestion, relaxation of the pylorus leading to lientery/diarrhea, remedies including hard egg yolks, nutmeg, and a closing powder, and symptoms of bilious heat versus cold phlegmatic matter in the stomach.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Illustration 1 (Canvas 214)** (`#xywh=850,500,2234,1800`): Red-wash anatomical illustration of the human stomach at the top of the left column, showing the esophagus entering from the upper right and the duodenum/pylorus curving to the upper left, set above a blank yellow-bordered cartouche.
